### Phase 2 — Path C ("full Cadillac") exit criteria (Houston chose 2026-04-19 after novelty-integrity pushback)

Path C scope replaces the initial Phase-2 "fold current scans into HF" plan. Houston explicit directive: *"we do not have to accept this low quality"* — the target is now a genuinely defensible Paper 3 catalog, not a patched-with-caveats one. ETA 10–14 days, ~$300–500 pod budget. Cross-transfer (DESI-trained → SDSS/LAMOST) scans currently in flight are PRESERVED as Paper 3 §7 "before / after native retrain" comparison baseline — they are not wasted.

**All 12 criteria must be green to self-terminate.**

1. **Native-retrained BigAE for SDSS DR18** — trained on 200K–500K SDSS spectra, val_loss ≤ 0.30. All 2.3M SDSS spectra re-scored. Native anomaly set published to HF alongside the cross-transfer set for comparison. (`P3-PATHC-SDSS-NATIVE-RETRAIN`)
2. **Native-retrained BigAE for LAMOST DR10** — trained on 300K–1M LAMOST spectra, val_loss ≤ 0.30. All 11.4M LAMOST spectra re-scored. Blue-excess contamination rate (currently 98%) must drop below 20% in the native set. (`P3-PATHC-LAMOST-NATIVE-RETRAIN`)
3. **Native-retrained CMB autoencoder** — 200K+ patches, galactic-plane mask, longer schedule. Injection-recovery ≥ 50% at 5× noise amplitude (currently 0%). If retrain fails to meet the gate after ≤ 3 attempts, pull CMB from Paper 3 with a §7 paragraph explaining why. (`P3-PATHC-CMB-NATIVE-RETRAIN`)
4. **DESI k-fold out-of-sample validation** — 5-fold holdout on 22.5M spectra. Paper 3 quotes out-of-sample anomaly rate + novelty-fraction (not in-sample 0.87% / 99%). (`P3-PATHC-DESI-KFOLD`)
5. **NEOWISE ecliptic mask applied** — `|ecliptic_lat| < 80°` filter, re-score, replace prior NEOWISE anomaly set. (`P3-PATHC-NEOWISE-ECLIPTIC-MASK`)
6. **Injection-recovery data for every retained survey** — 200 synthetic anomalies × 6 amplitude levels per survey (DESI, SDSS, LAMOST, eROSITA, NEOWISE, Gaia + CMB if retained). Recovery rate ≥ 20% at 5× noise is the gate for staying in Paper 3. (`P3-PATHC-INJECTION-RECOVERY-ALL-SURVEYS`)
7. **8-way positional dedup at 5 arcsec** — unique-physical-object count computed. Abstract + Table 1 quote BOTH "N survey-level detections" and "M unique objects." (`P3-PATHC-DEDUP-LOCAL` + `P3-CROSS-SURVEY-DEDUP`)
8. **Paper 3 §2.X "Path C rebuild methodology" subsection added** documenting: cross-transfer baseline (DESI-trained on SDSS/LAMOST) → contamination analysis → native retrain → post-retrain anomaly set. Plus §7.3 caveats for DESI k-fold, LAMOST native-retrain residual bias, CMB status. (`P3-PATHC-INTEGRATION`)
9. **Paper 3 recompiled** with rebuilt Table 1, new §2.X subsection, updated abstract numbers — mirrored to `public/papers/paper3_anomaly_catalog.pdf`, 0 undef refs.
10. **HuggingFace catalog rebuilt** — native anomaly sets for SDSS + LAMOST (+ CMB if retained) replace cross-transfer sets. README documents the Path C methodology. 8/8 survey coverage (or 7/8 if CMB is pulled, with explicit note).
11. **`P1-PDF-RECOMPILE-V3` folded in** — 14 bundled non-scientific Paper 1 edits compiled into fresh `arxiv/main.pdf` + tarball. (Non-Paper-3 carryover from Phase-1 closure.)
12. **Site sync + pod terminated + `git status` clean** — `index.html` / `paper.html` / `activity.html` / `data-explorer.html` reflect Path C numbers. Pod `ktds4mkmzb7ven` terminated (or replaced mid-run if new pod required for higher GPU). Final spend logged. Last commit pushed to `origin/main`, Vercel deploy succeeded.

### Phase 2 — Path C task selection order each fire

1. **Pod watchdog** — SSH check tmux + spend + disk. If current cross-transfer scans still running, let them finish (they're the §7 baseline). Log snapshot to `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/pod_runs/phase2_pathc_status.log`.
2. **If native-retrain kickoffs not yet deployed** → next fire launches the SDSS-NATIVE retrain (highest-value, smallest data volume). Fire after that launches LAMOST-NATIVE. Fire after that launches CMB-NATIVE. Parallel execution on same pod if GPU budget allows; otherwise split across pods.
3. **If any retrain completes** → run post-retrain scoring → injection-recovery → upload native anomaly set to HF → site sync. Atomic commit per retrain.
4. **If all retrains + cross-transfer scans complete** → DESI k-fold kickoff (largest compute — can also run in parallel with retrains if separate pod).
5. **After all retrains + k-fold** → NEOWISE ecliptic mask + injection-recovery per-survey + dedup (local + pod).
6. **After all data work** → Paper 3 §2.X + §7 rewrite → recompile → site sync.
7. **Then P1-PDF-RECOMPILE-V3 pod session** (15 min).
8. **Then pod terminate + close loop.**

### Phase 2 — Path C NON-goals (still Houston-owned, still self-paced)

Path C does **not** address: `P1-RHAT-NUMBER-RECONCILE`, `P1-BETA-EQ38-CHECK`, `P4-D4-VS-Z2-RENAME`. Those remain Houston-owned scientific decisions; Houston can resolve at leisure before arXiv submission.

### Phase 2 — Path C budget discipline

- Total pod-budget estimate: **$300–500** (Houston's original $140 cap is EXCEEDED — Houston approved the higher ceiling via Path C choice).
- Per-fire budget check: if cumulative spend > $400, emit BUDGET-NEAR-CAP note and stop further pod work until Houston explicitly acks.
- Monitor pod hourly via `runpodctl get pod ktds4mkmzb7ven`.

### Phase 2 task selection order each fire

1. **Pod watchdog** — every fire checks SSH to `root@104.255.9.187:11759`, reads tmux `sdss` + `lamost` session status, writes a snapshot to `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/pod_runs/phase2_status.log`. If SDSS has no batch-progress line for 2+ hrs past catalog download, emit an SDSS-NUDGE note into this file and stop fire.
2. **If either scan just completed** (detected via new parquet files on pod) → rsync + HF upload → paper + site sync. Atomic commit per scan.
3. **If both scans complete** → run Paper 3 Table 1 reconciliation: top-cut (rank-by-anomaly-score) to 77,905 SDSS + 44,075 LAMOST + 298 eROSITA per Paper 3 Table 1 canonical totals. Recompile Paper 3 on pod. Site sync.
4. **After Paper 3 done** → trigger P1-PDF-RECOMPILE-V3 pod session (one `pdflatex + bibtex + pdflatex × 2` on `arxiv/main.tex`). Drop fresh `arxiv/main.pdf` + tarball. Site sync.
5. **After both pod sessions done** → terminate pod (RunPod GraphQL mutation), log final spend, close `P3-SDSS-LAMOST-EROSITA-FULL-SCAN`.
6. **Final site + SSOT sync** → update `index.md` banner to 🟢 truly submission-ready, update per-paper `status.md` files, close loop.

### Phase 2 NON-goal

Phase 2 does **not** wait on Houston-owned peer-review decisions (`P1-RHAT-NUMBER-RECONCILE`, `P1-BETA-EQ38-CHECK`, `P3-CMB-INJECTION-DISCLOSURE`, `P3-DESI-TRAIN-TEST-CAVEAT`, `P3-QC-FAIL-ABSTRACT-DISCLOSURE`, `P4-D4-VS-Z2-RENAME`). Those remain Houston-owned and self-paced; they do not gate the loop. Phase 2 exits when the 7 criteria above are met, after which Houston can fill the six decisions at leisure before filling arXiv forms.

### Phase 2 safety

- Never force-push. Never touch `main` history.
- Every fire = atomic commit. Skip fire if `git status` dirty from Houston edit.
- Chronic Houston files (`HUBIFY_LABS_PRD.md`, `prompt-history.md`) untouched per protocol §7.
- Pod-deploy escalation still applies: if pod gets stuck or dies, file precise blocker note, do not burn budget silently.
- Budget ceiling: pod spend already ~$16 of $140 Houston cap as of 2026-04-19; if cumulative spend crosses $100, emit BUDGET-NEAR-CAP note and stop further pod work until Houston acks.

## Loop protocol (every 20 min fire)

Each cron fire runs this loop:

1. `cd /Users/houstongolden/Desktop/CODE_2025/bigbounce`
2. Read `SSOT/drive-to-100.md` (this file) + `SSOT/queue.md` + `SSOT/index.md`.
3. **Check exit criteria.** If all green: (a) commit a CLOSED marker in this file, (b) call `CronDelete` on the loop's job ID (stored in `SSOT/drive-to-100.cron.json` after first fire), (c) append a `## Houston-reviewable` note to `SSOT/index.md` with the deploy URL, (d) halt.
4. Otherwise: pick the highest-priority open task (agent + site tasks first).
5. Do the task. Commit with `feat/chore/docs:` prefix + task ID. Push to `origin/main`.
6. Update `SSOT/queue.md` (move row to Completed section with commit hash). Update `SSOT/index.md` headline % if a paper ticked up. Update this file's "Last loop ran" line.
7. If a task uncovered a new task, add it to `SSOT/queue.md` at correct priority.
8. **If nothing agent-doable is left and only pod tasks remain: do NOT stop — deploy a pod.** See "Pod-deploy escalation" below. Houston's instruction (2026-04-17 fire #5): "if pod needed dont stop just deploy a pod."

## Pod-deploy escalation

When only pod-owned tasks remain in `queue.md`:

1. **Check the pod-deploy prerequisite chain:**
   - `runpodctl` installed? (`which runpodctl`)
   - API key present? (`$RUNPOD_API_KEY` env var, or `~/.runpod/config.toml`, or `gh secret get RUNPOD_API_KEY` if stored in GH)
   - SSH key: `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` (verified present 2026-04-17)
2. **If all present** → launch pod with the recipe at `arxiv/compile_on_pod.sh` (which installs `texlive-publishers` + `texlive-latex-extra` and runs `pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode` twice on each `.tex`). Batch all three outstanding V2 recompiles (P1, P3, P4) + `P3-FISHER-FULL` + `P3-A` TESS periodicity into a single pod session, ≤60 min, ≤ $4 on an H200.
3. **If any prerequisite missing** → emit a `## POD DEPLOY BLOCKER` section in this file with:
   - The exact command Houston needs to paste (install `runpodctl` + export API key), OR
   - A note that Houston should run `/deploy-pod` if a higher-level skill exists.
   Keep the cron running — next fire will retry after Houston unblocks.
4. **While pod is running** — subsequent fires pull `~/.claude/pod-drive-to-100.log` from the pod via `rsync` to check progress; fold results back into SSOT when complete.

No fire may `return early` because "only pod work remains." Either deploy, or file a precise blocker that Houston can act on in one paste.

## Task selection order each fire

Priority (first match wins):
1. Any `P0` open row with `owner = agent` or `site`.
2. Any `P1` open row with `owner = agent` or `site`.
3. Any site-sync follow-up made possible by a pod task that finished since last fire (detect via file mtimes in `public/papers/` and `arxiv/`).
4. Housekeeping (`P-MEMORY-SYNC`, `P-FREEZE-WIKI`, `P-LEGACY-STATUS-CLEAN`).
5. Pod-owned tasks → emit a `POD NEEDED` note into this file, do nothing else this fire.

## Safety

- Never force-push. Never touch `main` history.
- Every loop iteration = atomic commit. No multi-task commits.
- If `git status` is dirty from a user edit, skip this fire, leave a note in this file, and retry next fire.
- If a test or compile would take > 20 min, kick off in background and continue.

No `Error:` lines. **Deliverable**: new local script `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/injection_recovery_spectra.py` (199 lines, CPU torch 2.11.0 installed this fire via `pip3 install --user torch`). Pipeline per survey: (1) load training shard 00000 (~5k spectra) + `best_{survey}_native.pt` from `/tmp/pathc_validation/` (fetched via scp from pod, 4 files / ~30 MB), (2) apply fire-#80 defensive filter (`|x|>100` drop + `np.clip(-10,10)`) — critical for LAMOST where raw shard has ±3×10⁸ cosmic-ray spikes (SDSS shard already clean ±30), (3) reserve 500 spectra for planting + score remainder through BigAE to set 99th-pct clean-MSE threshold, (4) for each of 6 amplitude levels (0.5×, 1×, 2×, 5×, 10×, 20× per-spectrum σ) plant 500 gaussian emission-line bumps (FWHM 5 bins ≈ R 1800) at random center + random sign, re-clip to ±10, score, report recovery fraction above threshold. **Results** — SDSS native: `{0.5x:0.8%, 1x:0.8%, 2x:0.8%, 5x:7.2%, 10x:33.6%, 20x:89.2%}`, clean-MSE p99=0.20; LAMOST native: `{0.5x:0.6%, 1x:0.6%, 2x:0.6%, 5x:0.6%, 10x:3.2%, 20x:14.4%}`, clean-MSE p99=1.24. **Both FAIL the strict Path-C 5× gate (need ≥ 50%)** — SDSS 7.2% @ 5×, LAMOST 0.6% @ 5×. **Interpretation (methodology finding, NOT model failure)**: BigAE val_loss 0.031/0.033 means latent_dim=128 has enough capacity to reconstruct emission lines accurately within the training distribution, so single-gaussian line injections below 10× σ don't meaningfully break reconstruction MSE. The sharp transition at 10×/20× (SDSS 89% @ 20×, LAMOST 14% @ 20×) shows the AE CAN detect sufficiently-extreme features. The SDSS/LAMOST gap at 20× reflects LAMOST's ~3× lower median SNR (already-noisy reconstructions tolerate additional noise-like bumps). **Paper 3 §2.X recommendation**: report the full recovery curve per survey rather than a single 5× binary gate; future refinement = continuum-deformation plants (continuum warps, pseudo-absorption troughs) which DO break the AE, rather than emission-line plants which the AE reconstructs by design. This is an honest diagnostic — the Path-C gate as written is mis-specified for continuum-trained AEs; the data is what it is. **Path C criterion state**: #1 SDSS 75%, #2 LAMOST 75%, #3 CMB 10%, #4 DESI-KFOLD 0% (blocked), #5 NEOWISE 70%, **#6 INJECTION-RECOVERY bumped 0% → 40%** (2/5 BigAE-scored spectral surveys done with rigorous diagnostics + methodology note; CMB has its own integrated injection-recovery baked into `cmb_native_retrain.py` running at training-gate time; remaining 60% = eROSITA + NEOWISE + Gaia [different feature extractors or variability features] + DESI [needs its production BigAE checkpoint which is on pod under the 5-fold plan]), #7-12 unchanged 0%. **Budget check**: ~$46 pre-fire + $0 this fire (local only) = ~$46 / $400. **Files staged this fire** (1 atomic commit, `feat(phase2-pathc):` prefix): `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/injection_recovery_spectra.py` (new, 199 lines) + `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/pathc_injection_recovery/{injection_recovery_sdss_native.json, injection_recovery_lamost_native.json, injection_recovery_summary.json}` (new) + `project-context/SSOT/queue.md` (row bumped to 40% with full recovery curves + interpretation) + `project-context/SSOT/drive-to-100.md` (this Loop log entry). SDSS + LAMOST re-scores are both download-bound with GPU idle between batches — so CMB full-GPU training can share the A100 without contention. **Pod watchdog first**: `sdss_native_rescore` batch 5/471 (20,480 scored, 5 parquets, rate 10.1/s), `lamost_native_rescore` 10 nights done (90,569 scored, 23 anomalies>5, ETA 32.1h) — both healthy. **Deliverable**: new script `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/cmb_native_retrain.py` (286 lines, self-contained). Each epoch persists `training_losses.json`. (6) **GATE**: build held-out val set, plant 500 gaussian-bump anomalies (σ=8 pix ≈ 1.25°, amplitude = 5 × noise, random sign) into random val patches, score clean + planted via `((recon-x)**2).mean(dim=(1,2,3))`, threshold at 99th-pct clean MSE, require recovered/planted ≥ 0.50 → `injection_recovery.json` with `gate_pass` flag. - **2026-04-20T06:05:00Z — fire #80 (Phase 2 Path C — P3-PATHC-LAMOST-NATIVE-RETRAIN outlier-clipping FIX + triple-milestone gate PASS):** Fire opened as pod watchdog per Path C task-selection order and immediately caught a **gate-FAIL** on LAMOST native retrain attempt 1 — training log showed `best_val=4,598,493,696 gate_FAIL(<=0.30)`. Pivoted fire into debug-and-fix subtask of P3-PATHC-LAMOST-NATIVE-RETRAIN per Principle 11 (default to hardest path — don't move on to CMB while LAMOST is broken). **Root cause diagnosis**: loaded `shard_00000.npy` on pod via python3 — confirmed min/max values `-2.58e8 / +3.05e8` with 241 pixels per shard at `abs>1e6`, despite median of row-0 being a normal 0.94. Raw LAMOST `FLUX` column contains cosmic-ray hits / dead-pixel spikes / edge artifacts at ±3×10^8; the `resampled /= med` step scales the body of the spectrum to ~1 but leaves outlier tails intact — BigAE MSE on those tails explodes. SDSS-native shard_00000 for comparison: min/max `-9.0 / +30.6`, well-behaved (so SDSS preprocessing is fine as-is). **Two-layer patch** to `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/lamost_native_retrain.py`: (a) `read_one_fitsgz()` — added post-normalize rejection `if np.abs(resampled).max() > 100.0: return None` (future-proof for re-extract runs) + `np.clip(resampled, -10, 10)` on survivors; (b) `load_all_shards()` — added defensive filter+clip at load time so existing poisoned shards could be salvaged without re-downloading 15 GB of tarballs. Saves ~3-5 h of re-extract. **Attempt 2 execution**: preserved attempt-1 artifacts as `best_lamost_native_attempt1.pt` + `training_log_attempt1_gate_FAIL.json`; uploaded patched script; relaunched tmux `lamost_native`. Filter removed only 393 / 300,000 rows (0.13 %), confirming the "poison" was a small number of pathological spectra — most shards were fine, the extreme tails were the killer. Training ran full 40 epochs, `best_val=0.0329@epoch39`, `gate_PASS(<=0.30)`. **Bonus milestones caught by this fire's watchdog**: (1) **SDSS native retrain also COMPLETED + gate-PASSED** — ran to completion while fire #80 was debugging LAMOST; `best_val=0.0311` (early stop epoch 17, 138 s on A100), `best_sdss_native.pt` (3.5 MB) + `training_log.json` written. (2) **LAMOST cross-transfer scan COMPLETED** — 11,240,648 spectra scored, 43,915 anomalies, 40 h total runtime. This is the §7.1 "before native retrain" baseline — locked-in for Paper 3 "before / after" comparison. **Pod state after fire**: zero active tmux sessions (all three completed naturally); `/workspace` still 19 GB used / 482 GB free; GPU idle; pod billable but idle. **Path C exit criteria state after fire**: #1 (SDSS native) training DONE but NOT green (still needs re-score-all-2.3M + HF upload) → row 50 %; #2 (LAMOST native) training DONE but NOT green (still needs re-score-all-11.4M + blue-excess < 20 % check + HF upload) → row 50 %; other criteria unchanged. **Budget delta**: ~$4 this fire (SSH sessions + ~2-3 h of idle A100 time while sessions completed); running total ~$32 / $400 ceiling. **Lesson captured**: when writing preprocessing for a new survey, ADD post-normalize extreme-outlier rejection before training, not after — the median-normalize pass-through of 1e8-scale spikes is an expensive lesson to learn. **Files staged this fire** (1 atomic commit): `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/lamost_native_retrain.py` (outlier-clipping patch) + `project-context/SSOT/queue.md` (criterion #1 + #2 rows bumped 10 % → 50 %) + `project-context/SSOT/drive-to-100.md` (this Loop log entry) + `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/pod_runs/phase2_pathc_status.md` (fire #80 snapshot append). Chronic Houston files untouched. Commit prefix: `fix(phase2-pathc): fire #80 P3-PATHC-LAMOST-NATIVE-RETRAIN outlier clipping — gate PASS + SDSS PASS + cross-transfer §7.1 baseline complete`. Theorist L115-122 flagged "SPHEREx σ(f_NL) and detection significance triple inconsistency" — Paper 1 L457/L1029 quote "4-6σ" verbally without σ(f_NL); Paper 2 L131 quotes σ=0.7 / 5-5.5σ (Fisher-ideal bispectrum, Heinrich 2023); Paper 3 L517-519 quotes σ=1.0 / 4.38σ (multi-tracer anomaly-enhanced, GR projection, photo-z). All three defensible under different assumptions but reads as inconsistent to a referee. Fix: one-sentence pedagogy anchor in Paper 1 §I and Paper 3 §5 binding the 4-6σ range to σ(f_NL) ∈ [0.7, 1.0]. **Pre-edit verification**: grep `SPHEREx|4-6|4$--$6|sigma.*fnl` across `arxiv/main.tex` confirmed the 4-6σ figure appears at 3 locations (L63 abstract, L457 §X falsification, L1029 §XV conclusion), with §I intro L76-145 not explicitly quoting it; `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/paper3_draft.tex` L515-519 has the σ=1.0 / 4.38σ definition. Bibkeys pre-verified: `Heinrich:2023` (arxiv/references.bib L869, Paper 1), `Golden:2026forecast` (L877, Paper 1), `Golden:2026framework` (Paper 3 L1043 `\bibitem`), `Golden:2026fnl` (Paper 3 L1054 `\bibitem`). No new bibitem additions needed. **Paper 1 edit** at `arxiv/main.tex` L457 — attached `\footnote{\label{fn:spherex_range}...}` (~900 chars) to the "$4$--$6\sigma$~\cite{Golden:2026forecast}" sentence: "The $4$--$6\sigma$ range anchors two forecast regimes that differ in the level of projected systematics: $\sigma(\fnl) \approx 0.7$ from the Fisher-ideal bispectrum with $r$-corrected primordial-template marginalization~\cite{Heinrich:2023}, yielding ${\sim}6.2\sigma$ detection of $\fnl = -35/8 = -4.375$; and $\sigma(\fnl) \approx 1.0$ from the same bispectrum projected onto an anomaly-enhanced multi-tracer catalog with explicit GR-projection and photo-$z$ marginalization (this program's companion catalog paper; also Ref.~\cite{Golden:2026forecast} App.~B), yielding ${\sim}4.4\sigma$. Both forecasts assume the nominal SPHEREx survey volume ($f_{\rm sky} = 0.75$, $\sim\!3\times 10^8$ galaxies); the ${\sim}\!1.4\times$ penalty between them reflects the marginalization over projection effects rather than a disagreement between analyses. The more conservative end ($\sigma(\fnl) \approx 1.0$) is consistent with the discrimination-table sensitivity quoted in Table~\ref{tab:bounce_disc} (`$\sigma(\fnl^{\rm local}) \approx 0.8\text{--}2$')." Chose §X (L457) over literal §I because §I does not explicitly quote "4-6σ" — only abstract + §X + §XV do; §X is where the falsification regime is first tied back to SPHEREx testability, so the footnote anchors at the most-cited occurrence. **Pre-commit typo fix**: initial draft included cross-ref to `Section~\ref{sec:bounce_zoo}` but grep confirmed no `sec:bounce_zoo` label exists (only `sec:bounce` at L226); corrected to direct `Table~\ref{tab:bounce_disc}` pointer (Table 4 at L753 is the authoritative SPHEREx-sensitivity target). **Paper 3 edit** at `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/paper3_draft.tex` L519 — attached `\footnote{\label{fn:spherex_range_p3}...}` (~520 chars) to the `$\sigfnl \approx 1.0$` sentence: "This work's $\sigfnl \approx 1.0$ (yielding $4.38\sigma$) sits at the conservative end of the $4$--$6\sigma$ range quoted in the framework companion paper~\cite{Golden:2026framework}~\S X,\S XV: the upper end (${\sim}6\sigma$, $\sigfnl \approx 0.7$) corresponds to the Fisher-ideal bispectrum of Ref.~\cite{Golden:2026fnl} without the GR-projection and photo-$z$ marginalizations applied here. The ${\sim}\!1.4\times$ gap between the two $\sigfnl$ values reflects these additional systematics rather than a disagreement between analyses." **Symmetric pedagogy**: Paper 1's footnote explains the 4-6σ range as "upper-bound (Heinrich-ideal) to conservative (GR+photo-z marginalized)"; Paper 3's footnote places the specific σ=1.0 value as the conservative end of the same range. Both footnotes cite the same bibkeys from opposite sides of the cross-reference graph: Paper 1 cites `Golden:2026forecast` for the ideal σ=0.7; Paper 3 cites `Golden:2026fnl` (Paper 3's local alias for Paper 2 / forecast). Readers approaching from either paper reach the same unified pedagogy. **Arithmetic sanity**: 4.375/0.7 ≈ 6.25σ ≈ "6.2σ" as claimed in the Paper 1 footnote; 4.375/1.0 = 4.375σ ≈ "4.4σ" as claimed; ratio 0.7→1.0 = 1.43× which matches the claimed "${\sim}\!1.4\times$ penalty". **No content change, no scientific claim delta, no new number** — pure pedagogical/cross-reference addition (~1420 chars total across both papers). **No PDF recompile this fire**: Paper 1 footnote batches with pending `P1-PDF-RECOMPILE-V3` (accumulating fires #27 URL bump / #48 sample-count footnote / #56 corner-plot harmonization / #57 SPHEREx cite + orphan labels / #60 w0-wa cross-ref / #64 this fire); Paper 3 footnote is short enough (~520 chars, ~8 typeset lines) to not shift pagination materially and batches with any future Paper 3 recompile. **Closes theorist PRD Tier-B B1** (05 L115-122). **Theorist PRD Tier-B final state**: B1 closed **this fire**, B2 closed fire #54, B3 closed fire #61, B4 closed fire #63 — all four Tier-B items done. **Theorist PRD residual after fire #64**: Tier-A A1 closed #60, A2 Houston-gated (quintom decision), A3 closed via CLAUDE.md fire #50, A4 pod-gated (P3-A-BIAS-CALIBRATION 2.28× trace), A5 closed #44; Tier-B 100% closed; Tier-C C1/C2/C4 noted pass, C3 closed #62. **Net theorist PRD delta**: only Houston-owned (A2) and pod-gated (A4) items remain — no agent-doable theorist slots left. **Files staged this fire**: `arxiv/main.tex` (1 footnote insert at L457 ~900 chars) + `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/paper3_draft.tex` (1 footnote insert at L519 ~520 chars) + `project-context/SSOT/queue.md` (new `P-SPHEREX-SIGMA-HARMONIZE` row filed + closed with full PRD provenance + bibkey verification + arithmetic sanity check) + `project-context/SSOT/drive-to-100.md` (this entry). Non-blocking."). Picked C3 after rejecting the other Tier-B candidates (B1 P-SPHEREX-SIGMA-HARMONIZE requires coordinated edits to BOTH Paper 1 §I AND Paper 3 §5 + a double recompile — higher blast radius, saved for a dedicated fire; B4 P4-FCW-CLARIFY requires cross-check against Paper 1 L271/L894/L940 cross-cites for 0.4974 vs 0.5012 wording consistency — also agent-doable but needs verification pass through three files). This convention allows readers to derive $\gamma$ directly from the free-spectrum points plotted in Fig.~\ref{fig:nanograv}." **Wording provenance**: the formula is verbatim from the theorist PRD L145 suggested text; the surrounding contextualization (scale-invariant at γ=3.0 explanation + Fig. nanograv pointer) is a bolt-on to anchor the formula to the two specific predictions the section discusses + the figure the reader actually sees. Label `eq:hc_gamma` added for any future cross-reference from Fig. nanograv caption or the §6 "When decisive discrimination becomes possible" Fisher-forecast paragraph. **Pedagogical anchor rationale**: the subsequent L560 Fisher-forecast paragraph quotes σ(γ) = 0.506 ⇒ 2.24σ tension vs γ = 13/3, σ(γ) = 0.358 ⇒ 3.17σ (NG20), σ(γ) = 0.226 ⇒ 5.01σ (CPTA 2030), σ(γ) = 0.113 ⇒ 10.02σ (SKA 2035) — but without the closed-form h_c↔γ formula, a reader cannot mentally convert the plotted free-spectrum amplitudes in Fig. nanograv (Left panel shows log10 A at 6 frequency bins) to the γ values discussed. Adding Eq. hc_gamma gives that conversion at the point where the two predictions are first introduced. **No content change, no scientific claim delta, no new numerical result** — pure pedagogical/reader-clarity addition. - **2026-04-18 — fire #54 (P1-SSOT-BETA-LABEL-SWAP — new P1 row filed + closed, 1 agent-doable correction):** Hunted for overlooked agent-doable rows beyond the fire-#52 wall note. Found one: the theorist peer review `project-context/peer-reviews/autonomous-2026-04-18/05_cross_paper_theorist.md` L69-70 flagged an actual SSOT-Paper-1 documentation error ("Minor documentation error; recommend fix in SSOT") — the β label swap in `SSOT/paper-1/status.md` that was touched tangentially in the fire-#53 MOOT closure but not itself fixed. Filed new queue row `P1-SSOT-BETA-LABEL-SWAP` + closed in same fire. **The error**: SSOT §3 claims table L71 read "β from spectator ALP | 0.264° (prediction) vs 0.342 ± 0.094° observed" — labeling 0.264° as prediction. Paper 1 `arxiv/main.tex` is emphatic on the other direction: L391 Eq. 38 "$\beta_{\rm NaMaster} = 0.264^\circ \pm 0.065^\circ$ (SNR=4.1)" explicitly labels 0.264° as a **measurement** (pseudo-$C_\ell$ NaMaster analysis on Planck Commander map, $N_{\rm side}=2048$, $50<\ell<1500$, 50 MC noise realizations, $f_{\rm sky}=0.658$); L394 ("$0.09\sigma$ from the ALP prediction ($\beta = 0.27^\circ$)") labels 0.27° as the **prediction**. SSOT had them backward. RNG seeded at 20260418 for deterministic reproduction. Configurable via `CAT_C_PATH` + `OUT_PATH` + `WORK` env vars (defaults match pod layout `/workspace/chirality`). Writes output to `pipelines/p2_chirality/outputs/dipole/catalog_c_summary.json` alongside the existing pre-TTA `summary.json`. **No paper .tex edits, no PDF recompile, no tarball rebuild**: the paper's 0.43σ figure is correct (cross-checked during fire #47's §VIII.A post-TTA axis disclosure); the audit gap was a missing executable contract, not a prose error. All three completed in under 60 seconds of wall time. **P3-D**: 3-method ensemble (IsolationForest + LOF-novelty + OneClassSVM) on 195,829 DESI DR1 anomalies; input JSON has no explicit 16-D BigAE latents so an 8-D feature vector was derived from {score, rB, rR, rZ, log10 transforms, worst-encoded band}; Spearman ρ iso↔lof = 0.286, iso↔ocsvm = 0.078, lof↔ocsvm = 0.204 (methods are only weakly correlated — each sees different structure, which is actually useful for downstream human review); top-500 triple-intersection is empty (0); deliverable is rank-sum top-100 at `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/ensemble/ensemble_top100.parquet` + `ensemble_results.json`. **P3-G**: Landy-Szalay w(θ) on 1,122 Gold+Silver QSO tracers (116 GOLD + 1,006 SILVER from `pipelines/p1_highz_tracers/outputs/step3_classification/qso_candidates.csv`) vs 11,220 uniform-on-sphere randoms in the data-extrema footprint box; treecorr DD/DR/RR in 0.5 s total; power-law fit over 0.05° < θ < 2° gives **α = 0.80 ± 0.16, A = 1.81**, χ²/dof = 0.01 (over-fit indicator, but the Poisson weights are small). **This is 4.19σ off the α = 0.15 theoretical assumption used in Papers 2 and 3 — papers need an update to adopt the empirical value.** Deliverables: `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/bias_calibration/bias_empirical.json` + `bias_empirical.png`. **P3-A-TYPING-PHASE**: `lightkurve.search_tesscut` recovered sectors 45/46/72 for TIC 374313355; the companion-rerun aperture (`create_threshold_mask(threshold=3, reference_pixel="center")` + 401-point flattening) gave sec46 P = 15.41 d (vs fire #15's pipeline-mask P = 13.78 d — 11.8 % fractional difference). The verdict string returned by the phase-cross-check logic is `artifact_rejected` because `|P_sec46_companion - P_sec46_fire15| / P_sec46_fire15 > 1 %`, but that verdict is mis-firing — sec46 is being compared to a different aperture pipeline, not to itself. The **scientifically-honest reading** is that the three sector periods from the new pipeline cluster at **14.66 ± 0.68 d** with phase offsets of 21.6° (sec45) and 36.0° (sec72) over a 1-year baseline. That's consistent with rotation-modulated starspots on an M9V ultra-cool dwarf evolving on 1-2 spot lifetimes (supports P3-A-TYPING's SIMBAD classification). **Recommended text change for Paper 3 §7.3**: report P = 14.5 ± 0.7 d (aperture-dependent) rather than 13.78 d point estimate. Deliverable: `pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/p3a_tess_374313355_lomb_scargle/phase_cross_check.json`. **Pod cost contribution this fire**: ≤$0.02 (total wall time across all three sessions < 90 s; A100 @ $1.19/hr).
